451.29065pt}(x)|x|^{j_{3}}\lesssim|x|^{r+j_{3}}|\hat{\tau}^{(i)}(x)|,

where the function on the right-hand side of the last inequality is integrable. The assertion follows.
∎

Lemma E.2
.

Suppose that
f_{0X}
satisfies the local Hölder condition (
4.3
) of Assumption
4.5

with
0<\upsilon\leq 1
and
L_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
.
For every
b\in\mathbb{R}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, if
h=o(1)
and
\delta=o(h)
, then

\int_{\mathbb{R}}|[e^{-bu}f_{0X}(x-u)-f_{0X}(x)]D^{j}H_{\delta}(u)|\,\mathrm{d}u\leq\frac{C_{j}}{\delta^{j}}\delta^{\upsilon}[L_{0}(x)+f_{0X}(x)],\quad x\in\mathbb{R},

(E.2)

where
C_{j}:=(3C_{\upsilon,j}\vee C_{1,j})>0
, with
C_{\upsilon,j}
as in (
E.1
).

Proof.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}
be fixed.
By Lemma
C.1
and the local Hölder condition (
4.3
) of Assumption
4.5
,
we have

\begin{split}&\delta^{j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|[e^{-bu}f_{0X}(x-u)-f_{0X}(x)]D^{j}H_{\delta}(u)|\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|[e^{-b\delta z}f_{0X}(x-\delta z)-f_{0X}(x)]H^{(j)}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|e^{-b\delta z}-1||f_{0X}(x-\delta z)-f_{0X}(x)||H^{(j)}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z\\
&\hskip 202.01474pt+f_{0X}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|e^{-b\delta z}-1||H^{(j)}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z\\
&\hskip 202.01474pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}}|f_{0X}(x-\delta z)-f_{0X}(x)||H^{(j)}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 3\int_{\mathbb{R}}|f_{0X}(x-\delta z)-f_{0X}(x)||H^{(j)}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z+f_{0X}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|e^{-b\delta z}-1||H^{(j)}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 3\delta^{\upsilon}L_{0}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|z|^{\upsilon}|H^{(j)}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z+b\delta f_{0X}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|z||H^{(j)}(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 3\delta^{\upsilon}C_{\upsilon,j}L_{0}(x)+b\delta C_{1,j}f_{0X}(x)<C_{j}\delta^{\upsilon}[L_{0}(x)+f_{0X}(x)].\end{split}

Inequality (
E.2
) follows.
∎

Lemma E.3
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
b=\mp\frac{1}{2}
and
\sigma=o(1)
, let the set
A_{b,\sigma}

be defined as in (
D.1
).
Under Assumptions
4.3
and

4.5
on
f_{0X}
, the latter with
0<\upsilon\leq 1
,
L_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and
any
R\geq 1
,
there exists a constant
\bar{C}_{m}>0
, depending on
m
and
\upsilon
, such that, for
\sigma
small enough,

\forall\,b=\mp\frac{1}{2},\quad A_{b,\sigma}^{c}\subseteq B_{\sigma},

(E.3)

with
B_{\sigma}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\,[L_{0}(x)+f_{0X}(x)]>\bar{C}_{m}^{-1}\sigma^{-\upsilon}f_{0X}(x)\}
.
Furthermore, there exist constants
C_{R},\,D_{R},\,S_{R}>0
, depending on
m
,
\upsilon
and
R
, so that

\|\bar{h}_{0,b}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}\|_{1}<C_{R}\sigma^{\upsilon R},\quad\|\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}\|_{1}<D_{R}\sigma^{\upsilon R}

(E.4)

and the function
f_{0X}+g_{b,\sigma}
, with
g_{b,\sigma}
as defined in (
D.2
), which is non-negative, has

1\leq\|f_{0X}+g_{b,\sigma}\|_{1}\leq 1+S_{R}\sigma^{\upsilon R}.

(E.5)

Proof.

Let
b
be fixed. We begin by proving the inclusion in (
E.3
).
Assume that
x\in A_{b,\sigma}^{c}
,
i.e.
,
\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)\leq-\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)/2
. Recall that

\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}=\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{(-\sigma^{2}/2)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{j=0}^{2k}\binom{2k}{j}(-b)^{2k-j}(\bar{h}_{0,b}\ast D^{j}H_{\delta}),

where, as in Lemma
4.1
, for constants
0<c_{\delta},\,c_{h}<1
, we
take
\delta:=c_{\delta}\sigma
and
h:=c_{h}|\log\sigma|^{-1/2}
.
Note that, by relationship (
6.9
),

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}}D^{j}H_{\delta}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u&=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}H(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{0\}}(j)\\
&=\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-16.36688pt\hbox{$(\tau\ast\phi_{h})$}\hskip-16.36688pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{0\}}(j)=(\tau\ast\phi_{h})(0)\mathbbm{1}_{\{0\}}(j)\leq 1.\end{split}

Then,

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}&\frac{(-\sigma^{2}/2)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{j=0}^{2k}\binom{2k}{j}(-b)^{2k-j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[\bar{h}_{0,b}(x-u)-\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)]D^{j}H_{\delta}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&\qquad=\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)-\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{(-\sigma^{2}/2)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{j=0}^{2k}\binom{2k}{j}(-b)^{2k-j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}D^{j}H_{\delta}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&\qquad\leq-\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)\left(\frac{1}{2}-(\tau\ast\phi_{h})(0)\frac{(b\sigma)^{2}}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{m-2}\frac{[-(b\sigma)^{2}/2]^{k}}{(k+1)!}\right)\\
&\qquad\leq-\frac{\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)}{2}[1-(b\sigma)^{2}]<-\frac{\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)}{4}.\end{split}

For
\sigma
small enough, by Lemma
E.2
,

\begin{split}&\hskip-2.84544pt\left|\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{(-\sigma^{2}/2)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{j=0}^{2k}\binom{2k}{j}(-b)^{2k-j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[\bar{h}_{0,b}(x-u)-\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)]D^{j}H_{\delta}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u\right|\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\frac{1}{M_{0X}(b)}\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{(\sigma^{2}/2)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{j=0}^{2k}\binom{2k}{j}|b|^{2k-j}e^{bx}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|e^{-bu}f_{0X}(x-u)-f_{0X}(x)||D^{j}H_{\delta}(u)|\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt<\frac{1}{M_{0X}(b)}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{[(1+|b|c_{\delta}\sigma)^{2}/(2c_{\delta}^{2})]^{k}}{k!}\max_{0\leq j\leq 2k}C_{j}\right)\sigma^{\upsilon}e^{bx}[L_{0}(x)+f_{0X}(x)]\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt<\frac{1}{M_{0X}(b)}\underbrace{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{(2/c_{\delta}^{2})^{k}}{k!}\max_{0\leq j\leq 2k}C_{j}\right)}_{=:\tilde{C}_{m}}\sigma^{\upsilon}e^{bx}[L_{0}(x)+f_{0X}(x)],\end{split}

where
0<\tilde{C}_{m}<\infty
.
Then, for
\bar{C}_{m}:=4\tilde{C}_{m}
, we have
A_{b,\sigma}^{c}\subseteq B_{\sigma}
.

We prove the inequalities in (
E.4
). Concerning the first one,

\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x<\sigma^{\upsilon R}\frac{\bar{C}_{m}^{R}}{M_{0X}(b)}\int_{B_{\sigma}}e^{bx}f_{0X}(x)\left(\frac{L_{0}}{f_{0X}}(x)+1\right)^{R}\mathrm{d}x\leq C_{R}\sigma^{\upsilon R},

where

C_{R}:=\frac{\bar{C}_{m}^{R}}{[M_{0X}(-1/2)\wedge M_{0X}(1/2)]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{|x|/2}f_{0X}(x)\left(\frac{L_{0}}{f_{0X}}(x)+1\right)^{R}\mathrm{d}x<\infty

by condition (
4.4
) and Assumption
4.3
.
As for the second inequality, from previous computations, for every
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have

\begin{split}&\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}|(\bar{h}_{0,b}\ast D^{j}H_{\delta})(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt<\delta^{-j}\left(\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\bar{h}_{0,b}(x-\delta u)-\bar{h}_{0,b}(x)||H^{(j)}(u)|\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}x+C_{0,j}C_{R}\sigma^{\upsilon R}\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\delta^{-j}\left(\frac{C_{j}\delta^{\upsilon}}{M_{0X}(b)}\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}e^{bx}[L_{0}(x)+f_{0X}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}x+C_{0,j}C_{R}\sigma^{\upsilon R}\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt<\delta^{-j}\left(\frac{C_{j}c_{\delta}^{\upsilon}\bar{C}_{m}^{R-1}}{M_{0X}(b)}\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}e^{bx}f_{0X}(x)\left(\frac{L_{0}}{f_{0X}}(x)+1\right)^{R}\mathrm{d}x+C_{0,j}C_{R}\right)\sigma^{\upsilon R}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\delta^{-j}C_{R}\left(\frac{C_{j}c_{\delta}^{\upsilon}}{\bar{C}_{m}}+C_{0,j}\right)\sigma^{\upsilon R},\end{split}

which, defined the constant

D_{R}:=C_{R}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}^{\upsilon}}{\bar{C}_{m}}+1\right)\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{(2/c_{\delta}^{2})^{k}}{k!}\max_{0\leq j\leq 2k}(C_{j}\vee C_{0,j}),

implies that

\|\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}\|_{1}<D_{R}\sigma^{\upsilon R}
.

To prove the last part of the lemma, we begin by noting that

f_{0X}+g_{b,\sigma}=[f_{0X}+M_{0X}(b)e^{-bx}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}]\mathbbm{1}_{A_{b,\sigma}}+\frac{1}{2}f_{0X}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}>\frac{1}{2}f_{0X}\geq 0

and

M_{0X}(b)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-bx}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=0.

In fact, since by Lemma
E.1
we have
\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-b\delta x}H(x)\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty
because
(\delta/h)=o(1)
, it holds that

\begin{split}&M_{0X}(b)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-bx}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\hskip 71.13188pt=\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{(-\sigma^{2}/2)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{j=0}^{2k}\binom{2k}{j}(-b)^{2k-j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-bu}D^{j}H_{\delta}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&\hskip 71.13188pt=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-b\delta x}H(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right)\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{(-\sigma^{2}/2)^{k}}{k!}\underbrace{\sum_{j=0}^{2k}\binom{2k}{j}(-b)^{2k-j}b^{j}}_{(-b+b)^{2k}=0}=0.\end{split}

Then, since

g_{b,\sigma}=M_{0X}(b)e^{-b\cdot}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}-[M_{0X}(b)e^{-b\cdot}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}+(f_{0X}/2)]\mathbbm{1}_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}
,
we have

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(f_{0X}+g_{b,\sigma})(x)\,\mathrm{d}x&=1+\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{0X}(b)e^{-bx}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x}_{=0}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}\left[M_{0X}(b)e^{-bx}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)+\frac{1}{2}f_{0X}(x)\right]\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=1-\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}\Big[\underbrace{M_{0X}(b)e^{-bx}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)}_{\leq-\frac{1}{2}f_{0X}(x)}+\frac{1}{2}f_{0X}(x)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\geq 1.\end{split}

On the other side, using Lemma
E.2
and reasoning as in the first part of the present lemma,

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(f_{0X}+g_{b,\sigma})(x)\,\mathrm{d}x&=1-\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}\left[M_{0X}(b)e^{-bx}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)+\frac{1}{2}f_{0X}(x)\right]\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=1-\frac{1}{2}\mu_{0X}(A_{b,\sigma}^{c})-M_{0X}(b)\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}e^{-bx}\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq 1+M_{0X}(b)\int_{A_{b,\sigma}^{c}}e^{-bx}|\gamma h_{m,b,\sigma}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq 1+\underbrace{[M_{0X}(-1/2)\vee M_{0X}(1/2)]D_{R}}_{=:S_{R}}\sigma^{\upsilon R}.\end{split}

Conclude that
1\leq\|f_{0X}+g_{b,\sigma}\|_{1}\leq 1+S_{R}\sigma^{\upsilon R}
. The proof is thus complete.
∎

Remark E.1
.

Although in condition (
4.3
) of Assumption
4.5
the constant

R\geq(2m/\upsilon)
, for the smallest integer
m\geq[2\vee(\alpha+2)/2]
, in Lemma
E.3

we have that
R
can be any real greater than or equal to
1
.

Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
5.1
on rate lower bounds

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

For clarity of exposition, we distinguish the cases where
d=1
and
d\geq 2
.

\bullet

Case
d=1

The proof develops along the lines of Theorem 3 in
[
dedecker:michel:2013
]
, pp. 281–285, and of Theorem 4.1 in
[
dedecker2015
]
, pp. 246–248. It uses intermediate results from
[
fan1991
]
, pp. 1267–1271, from Theorem 1 in
[
comtelacourihp
]
, pp. 577 and 590–594, and
from Theorem 2.10 in
[
MR3449779
]
, p. 10 and pp. 34–36.

We consider mixing distributions belonging to the class
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathbb{R},\,M)\cap\mathcal{S}(\alpha,\,L)
.
We begin by defining a finite family of Lebesgue absolutely continuous probability measures on
\mathbb{R}
,
with uniformly bounded first moments, whose densities belong to
\mathcal{S}(\alpha,\,L)
.
For
1<r<\frac{3}{2}
, we define the density

f_{0,r}(x):=C_{r}(1+x^{2})^{-r},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}.

(F.1)

Let
H(\cdot)
be the kernel function on
\mathbb{R}
defined in
[
fan1991
]
, p. 1268, which is such that, in particular,

•

H
is real, bounded and continuous,

•

H(0)\neq 0
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}H(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=0
and
\int_{0}^{1}|H^{(-1)}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x>0
, where
H^{(-1)}(x):=\int_{-\infty}^{x}H(u)\,\mathrm{d}u
is a primitive of
H
,

•

|H(x)|\leq c(1+x^{2})^{-\delta}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
, with
\delta>\frac{3}{2}
,

•

\hat{H}(t)=0
(hence also
\hat{H}^{(1)}(t)=\hat{H}^{(2)}(t)=0
),
|t|\notin[1,\,2]
.

Let
b_{n}:=([n^{1/[2(\alpha+\beta)+1]}]\vee 1)
, where
[\cdot]
denotes the integer part.
For
\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}
and
C>0
, let

f_{\bm{\theta}}(x):=f_{0,r}(x)+Cb_{n}^{-\alpha}\sum_{s=1}^{b_{n}}\theta_{s}H(b_{n}(x-x_{s,n})),\quad x\in\mathbb{R},

(F.2)

where
x_{s,n}:=(s-1)/b_{n}
. Defined the measure

\mu_{\bm{\theta}}:=f_{\bm{\theta}}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda
,
we show that

\{\mu_{\bm{\theta}}:\,{\bm{\theta}}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{1}.

The function
f_{\bm{\theta}}
is a density

Since
f_{0,r}
is a density and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}H(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=0
, we have
\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{\bm{\theta}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1
.
To ensure that
f_{\bm{\theta}}\geq 0
, it is sufficient to show that
|f_{\bm{\theta}}-f_{0,r}|\leq f_{0,r}
.
In fact, by Lemma 7 in
[
fan-truong
]
, pp. 1923–1924, since
\delta>(r\vee\frac{1}{2})
, there exists
\tilde{C}>0
such that, for
n
large enough, we have

f_{0,r}^{-1}(x)|f_{\bm{\theta}}(x)-f_{0,r}(x)|=CC_{r}^{-1}(1+x^{2})^{r}b_{n}^{-\alpha}\sum_{s=1}^{b_{n}}\theta_{s}|H(b_{n}(x-x_{s,n}))|\leq CC_{r}^{-1}\tilde{C}b_{n}^{-\alpha}\leq 1.

The probability measure
\mu_{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathbb{R},\,M)

Let
\mu_{0,r}:=f_{0,r}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda
. Since
r>1
we have
M_{1}(\mu_{0,r}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|x|f_{0,r}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty
. For
n
large enough, since
\delta>1
, we have

\begin{split}M_{1}(\mu_{\bm{\theta}})&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|x|f_{\bm{\theta}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|x|f_{0,r}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x+Cb_{n}^{-\alpha}\sum_{s=1}^{b_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|x||H(b_{n}(x-x_{s,n}))|\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq M_{1}(\mu_{0,r})+cCb_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\sum_{s=1}^{b_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{\delta}}\left(\frac{|u|}{b_{n}}+x_{s,n}\right)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&=M_{1}(\mu_{0,r})+cCb_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{\delta}}\left(|u|+\sum_{s=1}^{b_{n}}x_{s,n}\right)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&<M_{1}(\mu_{0,r})+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|u|+1}{(1+u^{2})^{\delta}}\,\mathrm{d}x=:M_{0,r,\delta}<\infty.\end{split}

Thus,
\mu_{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathbb{R},\,M)
for every
M\geq M_{0,r,\delta}
.

The density
f_{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{S}(\alpha,\,L)

By Lemma 4 in
[
comtelacourihp
]
, p. 590, we have

\hat{f}_{0,r}(t)=\exp{(-|t|^{2r-1})}
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
, where
0<(2r-1)<2
.
Let
L_{0,r,\alpha}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+t^{2})^{\alpha}|\hat{f}_{0,r}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t<\infty
.
For
n
large enough, we have

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+t^{2})^{\alpha}|\hat{f}_{\bm{\theta}}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t&\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+t^{2})^{\alpha}\big[|\hat{f}_{0,r}(t)|^{2}+C^{2}b_{n}^{-2(1+\alpha)}|\hat{H}(t/b_{n})|^{2}\big]\,\mathrm{d}t\\
&\leq 2\big[L_{0,r,\alpha}+C^{2}b_{n}^{-1}(4+b_{n}^{-2})^{\alpha}\|\hat{H}\|_{2}^{2}\big]<2L_{0,r,\alpha}+1=:L_{0}.\end{split}

Thus,
f_{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{S}(\alpha,\,L)
for every
L\geq L_{0}
.

The rest of the proof proceeds along the lines of Theorem 3 in
[
dedecker:michel:2013
]
, pp. 281–285. Therefore, we only sketch it. Let
\hat{\mu}_{n}
be an estimator of
\mu
based on the sample
Y^{(n)}
.
Let
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}
be a random vector whose components are i.i.d. Bernoulli random variables
\tilde{\theta}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\tilde{\theta}_{b_{n}}
, with
P(\tilde{\theta}_{s}=1)=P(\tilde{\theta}_{s}=0)=\frac{1}{2}
, for
s\in[b_{n}]
. We have

\begin{split}\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)&\geq\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu_{\bm{\theta}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu_{\bm{\theta}})\\
&\geq\inf_{{\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.42194pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}}_{n}}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu_{\bm{\theta}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}W_{1}(\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n},\,\mu_{\bm{\theta}})\\
&\geq\inf_{{\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.42194pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}}_{n}}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}W_{1}(\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n},\,\mu_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}})\\
&=\inf_{{\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.42194pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}|\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle F$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}}}\smash{\hat{F}}}_{n}(x)-F_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

where the infimum is taken over all estimators
{\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}}_{n}
of
\mu_{\bm{\theta}}
,
the expectation
\mathbb{E}
is taken with respect to the distribution of
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}

and
\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle F$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}}}\smash{\hat{F}}}_{n},\,F_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}
are the distribution functions of

\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n}
and
\mu_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}
, respectively. For
\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}
and
s\in[b_{n}]
, we define the densities

f_{\bm{\theta},s,u}:=f_{(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{s-1},u,\theta_{s+1},\ldots,\theta_{b_{n}})},\quad u=0,\,1,

and let
\mu_{\bm{\theta},s,u}:=f_{\bm{\theta},s,u}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda
, for
u=0,\,1
, be the corresponding probability measures on
\mathbb{R}
. Let
h_{\bm{\theta},s,u}
be the density of

\mu_{\bm{\theta},s,u}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}
, for
u=0,\,1
.
Using a standard randomization argument,
it can be shown that there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that

\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)\geq C_{1}b_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\int_{0}^{1}|H^{(-1)}(u)|\,\mathrm{d}u\gtrsim n^{-(\alpha+1)/[2(\alpha+\beta)+1]},

provided that
[1-\chi^{2}(h_{\bm{\theta},s,0};\,h_{\bm{\theta},s,1})/2]^{2n}
is bounded below by a constant, where the
\chi^{2}
-divergence between two densities
h_{0}
and
h_{1}
on
\mathbb{R}
is defined as

\chi^{2}(h_{0};\,h_{1})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{[h_{0}(x)-h_{1}(x)]^{2}}{h_{0}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Using standard arguments from
[
fan1991
]
, see the proof of Theorem 5, pp. 1269–1271, under assumption (
5.1
), it can be shown that there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
so that

\chi^{2}(h_{\bm{\theta},s,0};\,h_{\bm{\theta},s,1})\leq C_{2}b_{n}^{-[2(\alpha+\beta)+1]}\leq C_{2}n^{-1}
.

We now show that also the sequence
n^{-1/2}
is a lower bound on
\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)
.
In fact, replacing the function in (
F.2
) with

f_{\theta}(x):=f_{0,r}(x)+Ca_{n}^{-1}\theta H(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R},\quad\theta\in\{0,1\},

and taking
a_{n}=([n^{1/2}]\vee 1)
, all previous steps go through and we find that, for a constant
C_{3}>0
,

\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)\geq C_{3}a_{n}^{-1}\gtrsim n^{-1/2}

and
\chi^{2}(f_{0}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon};\,f_{1}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})\lesssim a_{n}^{-2}\lesssim n^{-1}
.
Combining the two previously obtained bounds, we conclude that

\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)\gtrsim\max\{n^{-(\alpha+1)/[2(\alpha+\beta)+1]},\,n^{-1/2}\}=n^{-(\alpha+1)/[2\alpha+(2\beta\vee 1)+1]}.

\bullet

Case
d\geq 2

We define the
d\times d
matrix

\mathbf{A}=\left(\begin{array}[]{l@{}l}1&\quad\mathbf{1}^{t}\\
\mathbf{0}&\quad\mathbf{I}_{d-1}\\
\end{array}\right),

where
\mathbf{0}
is a
(d-1)\times 1
-column vector with all elements equal to
0
,
while
\mathbf{1}^{t}
is a
1\times(d-1)
-row vector with all elements equal to
1
and

\mathbf{I}_{d-1}
is the identity matrix of size
(d-1)
.
The matrix
\mathbf{A}
is invertible and, being upper triangular, the determinant is the product of the main diagonal entries, therefore
\mathrm{det}(\mathbf{A})=1
.
For each observation
\mathsf{Y}_{i}
, we consider the transformation

\mathsf{Z}_{i}:=\mathbf{A}\mathsf{Y}_{i}=\mathbf{A}(\mathsf{X}_{i}+\bm{\varepsilon}_{i})=\mathbf{A}\mathsf{X}_{i}+\mathbf{A}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i},\quad i\in[n].

Set the position

\bm{\eta}_{i}:=\mathbf{A}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i},

the
d\times 1
-column vector
\bm{\eta}_{i}
has elements

\eta_{i,j}=\begin{cases}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\varepsilon_{i,k},&if $j=1$,\\
\varepsilon_{i,j},&if $j=2,\,\ldots,\,d$\,.\end{cases}

The random variables
\eta_{1,1},\,\ldots,\,\eta_{n,1}
are i.i.d. according to the
d
-fold convolution measure
\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\ast d}
, that is,

\mu_{\eta_{i,1}}=\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\ast d},\quad i\in[n].

We now show that condition (
5.1
) implies that, for every
l=0,\,1,\,2
,

|\hat{\mu}_{\eta_{i,1}}^{(l)}(t)|\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-(\beta d+l)},\quad t\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mbox{for }i\in[n].

(F.3)

In fact, by condition (
5.1
) with
l=0
,

|\hat{\mu}_{\eta_{i,1}}(t)|=|[\hat{\mu}_{\varepsilon}(t)]^{d}|\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-\beta d},\quad t\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mbox{for }i\in[n].

By the same condition, with
l=1
,

\begin{split}|\hat{\mu}_{\eta_{i,1}}^{(1)}(t)|&=d|\hat{\mu}_{\varepsilon}(t)|^{d-1}|\hat{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}(t)|\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-(\beta d+1)},\quad t\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mbox{for }i\in[n],\end{split}

and, with
l=2
,

\begin{split}|\hat{\mu}_{\eta_{i,1}}^{(2)}(t)|&=|d(d-1)[\hat{\mu}_{\varepsilon}(t)]^{d-2}[\hat{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}(t)]^{2}+d[\hat{\mu}_{\varepsilon}(t)]^{d-1}\hat{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}(t)|\\
&\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-\beta(d-2)}(1+|t|)^{-2(\beta+1)}+(1+|t|)^{-\beta(d-1)}(1+|t|)^{-(\beta+2)}\\
&\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-(\beta d+2)},\quad t\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mbox{for }i\in[n].\end{split}

This proves that condition (
F.3
) holds.

We make a preliminary remark for bounding below the supremum of the
L^{1}
-Wasserstein risk.
For a random vector
\mathsf{X}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with distribution
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\,M)
, we denote by
\mu^{\mathbf{A}}
the distribution of the transformation
\mathbf{A}\mathsf{X}
, which is the image measure of
\mu
by
{\mathbf{A}}
. Let
\hat{\mu}_{n}
be any estimator of
\mu
based on the observations

\mathsf{Y}^{(n)}=(\mathsf{Y}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\mathsf{Y}_{n})
.
We denote by
\hat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathbf{A}}
the corresponding estimator of
\mu^{\mathbf{A}}
,
which is a function of
\mathsf{Z}^{(n)}=(\mathsf{Z}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\mathsf{Z}_{n})
, with

\mathsf{Z}_{i}={\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{Y}_{i}
, for
i\in[n]
. Then,

\begin{split}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathbf{A}},\,\mu^{\mathbf{A}})&=\inf_{\tau\in\Gamma(\hat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathbf{A}},\,\mu^{\mathbf{A}})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{z}^{\prime}|\,\tau(\mathrm{d}\mathsf{z},\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{z}^{\prime})\\
&=\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{y}-{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{y}^{\prime}|\,\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mathsf{y},\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{y}^{\prime})\\
&=\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathbf{A}(\mathsf{y}-\mathsf{y}^{\prime})|\,\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mathsf{y},\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{y}^{\prime})\leq|{\mathbf{A}}|W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu),\end{split}

where
|{\mathbf{A}}|:=(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}a_{ij}^{2})^{1/2}=\sqrt{2d-1}
. Therefore,

|{\mathbf{A}}|W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)\geq W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathbf{A}},\,\mu^{\mathbf{A}}).

(F.4)

We now study the supremum of the
L^{1}
-Wasserstein risk over the class
\mathcal{D}_{d}=\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\,M)\cap\mathcal{S}_{d}(\alpha,\,L)
. We begin by defining a finite family of Lebesgue absolutely continuous probability measures on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, with uniformly bounded first moments, whose densities belong to
\mathcal{S}_{d}(\alpha,\,L)
.
Let
b_{n}:=([n^{1/[2(\alpha+\beta d)+1]}]\vee 1)
.
Let
f_{0,r}
be the density defined in (
F.1
) and
\mu_{0,r}=f_{0,r}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda
the corresponding probability measure.
For
\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}
, let
\mu_{\bm{\theta}}=f_{\bm{\theta}}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda
be the probability measure corresponding to the density
f_{\bm{\theta}}
defined in (
F.2
).
Define the product probability measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}

\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}}:=\mu_{\bm{\theta}}\otimes\mu_{0,r}^{\otimes(d-1)}=(f_{\bm{\theta}}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda)\otimes(f_{0,r}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda)\otimes\ldots\otimes(f_{0,r}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda)

having Lebesgue density
\bar{f}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}}(\tilde{\mathsf{x}})=f_{\bm{\theta}}(\tilde{x}_{1})\times\prod_{j=2}^{d}f_{0,r}(\tilde{x}_{j})
,
\tilde{\mathsf{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Define
\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}
to be the distribution of
\mathsf{X}:=\mathbf{A}^{-1}\tilde{\mathsf{X}}
when
\tilde{\mathsf{X}}\sim\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}}
. In other words,
\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}
has density

\bar{f}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathsf{x})=\bar{f}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbf{A}\mathsf{x})
.
We show that

\{\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}:\,{\bm{\theta}}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{d}.

The probability measure
\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\,M)

Taking into account that the Euclidean norm of any vector is bounded above by its
1
-norm, that is,
|\mathsf{x}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}|x_{j}|
,
for
n
large enough we have

\begin{split}M_{1}(\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}})&\leq|\mathbf{A}^{-1}|M_{1}(\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}})\leq|\mathbf{A}^{-1}|\left[M_{1}(\mu_{\bm{\theta}})+\sum_{j=2}^{d}M_{1}(\mu_{0,r})\right]=:\bar{M}_{0,r,\delta}<\infty\end{split}

by the same arguments laid down for the case
d=1
.
Thus,
\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\,M)
for every
M\geq\bar{M}_{0,r,\delta}
.

The density
\bar{f}_{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{S}_{d}(\alpha,\,L)

First note that

\hat{\bar{f}}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathsf{t})=\hat{\bar{f}}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}}(\mathsf{t}\cdot\mathbf{A}^{-1})
.
By the same arguments exposed for the case
d=1
, for
n
large enough we have

\begin{split}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\hat{\bar{f}}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathsf{t})|^{2}(1+t_{j}^{2})^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{t}=&\sum_{j=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\hat{\bar{f}}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}}(\mathsf{t}\cdot\mathbf{A}^{-1})|^{2}(1+t_{j}^{2})^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{t}\\
&\leq d\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\hat{\bar{f}}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}}(\mathsf{t}\cdot\mathbf{A}^{-1})|^{2}(1+|\mathsf{t}|^{2})^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{t}\\
&<d|\mathbf{A}|^{2\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\hat{\bar{f}}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}}(\mathsf{t})|^{2}(1+|\mathsf{t}|^{2})^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{t}\\
&=d|\mathbf{A}|^{2\alpha}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\hat{f}_{\bm{\theta}}(t_{1})|^{2}(1+t_{1}^{2})^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\sum_{j=2}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\hat{f}_{0,r}(t_{j})|^{2}(1+t_{j}^{2})^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t_{j}\bigg)=:L_{0}.\end{split}

Therefore, there exists a finite constant
\bar{L}_{0}>0
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\hat{\bar{f}}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathsf{t})|^{2}(1+t_{j}^{2})^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{t}\leq\bar{L}_{0}
.
Thus,
\bar{f}_{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{S}_{d}(\alpha,\,L)
for every
L\geq\bar{L}_{0}
.

Let
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}
be a random vector whose components are i.i.d. Bernoulli random variables
\tilde{\theta}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\tilde{\theta}_{b_{n}}
, with
P(\tilde{\theta}_{s}=1)=P(\tilde{\theta}_{s}=0)=\frac{1}{2}
, for
s\in[b_{n}]
. By the inequality in (
F.4
),
for any estimator
\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n}
that is a measurable function of the observations
\mathsf{Z}^{(n)}

from
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{n}
into the set of probability measures on
\mathbb{R}
, we have

\begin{split}|{\mathbf{A}}|\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{d}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)&\geq|{\mathbf{A}}|\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}})\\
&\geq\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathbf{A}},\,\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}})\\
&\geq\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}((\hat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1},\,(\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1})\\
&\geq\inf_{\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.42194pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n}}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}(\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n},\,(\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1})\\
&\geq\inf_{\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.42194pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n}}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}(\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n},\,(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1})\\
&=\inf_{\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.42194pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}|\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle F$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}}}\smash{\hat{F}}}_{n}(x)-F_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

where
\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle F$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}}}\smash{\hat{F}}}_{n}
is the distribution function of
\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n}
and

F_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}
is the distribution function of

(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}
, which is the marginal distribution of
\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}^{\mathbf{A}}
on the first coordinate, that is,
\mu_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}
, whose density is

f_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}=f_{0,r}+Cb_{n}^{-\alpha}\sum_{s=1}^{b_{n}}\tilde{\theta}_{s}H(b_{n}(\cdot-x_{s,n})).

For
\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}
and
s\in[b_{n}]
, we define the densities

\bar{f}_{\bm{\theta},s,u}:=\bar{f}_{(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{s-1},u,\theta_{s+1},\ldots,\theta_{b_{n}})},\quad\mbox{for }u=0,\,1,

and let
\bar{\mu}_{\bm{\theta},s,u}:=\bar{f}_{\bm{\theta},s,u}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda
, for
u=0,\,1
, be the corresponding probability measures on
\mathbb{R}
.
For any
x\in[x_{s,n},\,x_{s+1,n}]
, taking the expected value with respect to
\tilde{\theta}_{s}
and using the subscript

\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\setminus s:=(\tilde{\theta}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\tilde{\theta}_{s-1},\,\tilde{\theta}_{s+1},\,\ldots,\,\tilde{\theta}_{b_{n}})
to denote the expected value with respect to the remaining
components of the vector
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}|\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle F$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}}}\smash{\hat{F}}}_{n}(x)-F_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}(x)|\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\setminus s}\bigg[\sum_{u=0}^{1}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,u}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}|\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle F$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}}}\smash{\hat{F}}}_{n}(x)-F_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,u}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}(x)|\bigg]\\
&\quad\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\setminus s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\ldots\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|F_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,0}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}(x)-F_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,1}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}(x)|\\
&\qquad\quad\times\min\bigg\{\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\bar{f}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,0}\ast f_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})^{\mathbf{A}}(\mathsf{z}_{i}),\,\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\bar{f}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,1}\ast f_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})^{\mathbf{A}}(\mathsf{z}_{i})\bigg\}\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{z}_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathsf{z}_{n}\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{2}b_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\big|H^{(-1)}(b_{n}(x-x_{s,n}))\big|\\
&\qquad\quad\times\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\setminus s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\min\bigg\{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,0}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}}\big)(z_{i,1}),\,\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,1}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}}\big)(z_{i,1})\bigg\}\,\mathrm{d}{z_{1,1}}\ldots\mathrm{d}{z_{n,1}}\\
&\quad\geq\frac{1}{4}b_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\big|H^{(-1)}(b_{n}(x-x_{s,n}))\big|\\
&\qquad\quad\times\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\setminus s}\bigg[1-\frac{1}{2}\chi^{2}\Big(f_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,0}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}};\,f_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}},s,1}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}}\Big)\bigg]^{2n}\end{split}

because the following facts hold:

•

for any
\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}
,

\begin{split}|F_{(\bar{\mu}_{{\bm{\theta}},s,0}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}(x)-F_{(\bar{\mu}_{{\bm{\theta}},s,1}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}(x)|&=Cb_{n}^{-\alpha}\bigg|\int_{-\infty}^{x}(f_{\bm{\theta},s,0}-f_{\bm{\theta},s,1})(u)\,\mathrm{d}u\bigg|\\
&=Cb_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{x}H(b_{n}(u-x_{s,n}))\,\mathrm{d}u\right|\\
&=Cb_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\big|H^{(-1)}(b_{n}(x-x_{s,n}))\big|;\end{split}

•

for any
\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}
and
u=0,\,1
, all observations
{z_{1,1}},\,\ldots,\,{z_{n,1}}
are i.i.d. according to the probability measure

((\bar{\mu}_{{\bm{\theta}},s,u}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}=((\bar{\mu}_{{\bm{\theta}},s,u})^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}\ast((\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}=\mu_{\bm{\theta},s,u}\ast\mu_{\eta_{1,1}};

•

by the same arguments as in Theorem 3 of
[
dedecker:michel:2013
]
, p. 283, for any
\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}
,

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\min\bigg\{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{\bm{\theta},s,0}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}}\big)(z_{i,1}),\,\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{\bm{\theta},s,1}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}}\big)(z_{i,1})\bigg\}\,\mathrm{d}{z_{1,1}}\ldots\mathrm{d}{z_{n,1}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg[1-\frac{1}{2}\chi^{2}\Big(f_{\bm{\theta},s,0}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}},f_{\bm{\theta},s,1}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}}\Big)\bigg]^{2n}.\end{split}

By applying the same arguments as in
[
fan1991
]
, p. 1270,
with the difference that the error density is ordinary smooth of order
\beta d
instead of
\beta
and condition (
F.3
) holds, we get that there exists a constant
c>0
such that, for any
\bm{\theta}\in\{0,\,1\}^{b_{n}}
, we have

\chi^{2}\Big(f_{\bm{\theta},s,0}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}},f_{\bm{\theta},s,1}\ast f_{\eta_{1,1}}\Big)\leq cb_{n}^{-[2(\alpha+\beta d)+1]}\lesssim n^{-1}.

Thus, for a suitable constant
C^{\prime}>0
,

\begin{split}|{\mathbf{A}}|\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{d}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)&\geq\inf_{\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{1.05487pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.60275pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle\mu$}}}{\raisebox{0.42194pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}}}\smash{\hat{\mu}}}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}|\hat{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\displaystyle F$}}}{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{\vphantom{$\textstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptstyle F$}}}{\raisebox{0.60277pt}{\vphantom{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}}}\smash{\hat{F}}}_{n}(x)-F_{(\bar{\mu}_{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}^{\mathbf{A}})_{1}}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\geq C^{\prime}b_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\sum_{s=1}^{b_{n}}\int_{x_{s,n}}^{x_{s+1,n}}\big|H^{(-1)}(b_{n}(x-x_{s,n}))\big|\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=C^{\prime}b_{n}^{-(\alpha+1)}\int_{0}^{1}\big|H^{(-1)}(u)\big|\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&\gtrsim n^{-(\alpha+1)/[2(\alpha+\beta d)+1]}.\end{split}

To show that also the sequence
n^{-1/2}
is a lower bound on
\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{d}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)
we can reason as in the case
d=1
.
Therefore, combining the two previously obtained bounds, we have that

\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{d}}\mathbb{E}^{n}_{(\mu\ast\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes d})}W_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{n},\,\mu)\gtrsim\max\{n^{-(\alpha+1)/[2(\alpha+\beta d)+1]},\,n^{-1/2}\}=n^{-(\alpha+1)/[2\alpha+(2\beta d\vee 1)+1]}

and the proof is complete.
∎

Appendix G
Proof of Theorem
5.2
and related results

G.1
Proof of Theorem
5.2

We first note that

\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\|F_{\tilde{\mu}_{1n,\mathsf{v}}}-F_{\mu_{0X,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}\leq\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\|F_{\tilde{\mu}_{n,\mathsf{v}}}-F_{\mu_{0X,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}+O(n^{-1/2}).

Then, by inequality (
2.4
) and Theorem
3.1
with
\beta>1
and any sequence
h_{n}\rightarrow 0
,

\begin{split}W_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{1n},\,\mu_{0X})&\leq C_{d}\overline{W}_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{1n},\,\mu_{0X})\\
&=C_{d}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\|F_{\tilde{\mu}_{1n,\mathsf{v}}}-F_{\mu_{0X,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}\\
&\lesssim\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\|F_{\tilde{\mu}_{n,\mathsf{v}}}-F_{\mu_{0X,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}+n^{-1/2}\\
&\lesssim h_{n}+\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\|F_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}-F_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}+n^{-1/2}\\
&\qquad\,\,+(\log n)\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\bigg(h_{n}^{-\beta|I^{*}_{h_{n}}(\mathsf{v})|+1}\prod_{j\in I^{*}_{h_{n}}(\mathsf{v})}|v_{j}|^{\beta}\|f_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}-f_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}\bigg).\end{split}

We now bound
\|f_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}-f_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}
uniformly in
\mathsf{v}
. In
[
comtelacourihp
]
, the authors control the errors in the
L^{2}
-distance, therefore we need to control the above term differently.
For every
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, with
\mathbb{G}_{n}:=\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0Y})
the empirical process, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}-f_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg(\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath tx}\hat{\mu}_{0Y}(t\mathsf{v})[1-\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})]\,\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath tx}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\frac{\mathbb{G}_{n}(e^{\imath t\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}})}{\sqrt{n}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right|\bigg)\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg].

We denote by
B_{1}(\mathsf{v})
the first integral and by
B_{2}(\mathsf{v})
the second one.
In Section
G.2
, we show that

B_{1}(\mathsf{v})\lesssim b_{n}^{2|I^{*}_{b_{n}}(\mathsf{v})|}\prod_{j\in I^{*}_{b_{n}}(\mathsf{v})}v_{j}^{-2}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\bigg[\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}B_{2}(\mathsf{v})\bigg]\lesssim\frac{(\log n)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{nb_{n}}}.

(G.1)

Since
\beta=2
, choosing
h_{n}=b_{n}=[n/(\log n)^{3}]^{-1/(4d+1)}
, the bounds in (
G.1
) imply that

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\bigg(b_{n}^{-2|I^{*}_{b_{n}}(\mathsf{v})|+1}\prod_{j\in I^{*}_{b_{n}}(\mathsf{v})}v_{j}^{2}\|f_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}-f_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}\bigg)&\lesssim b_{n}+\frac{b_{n}^{-2d+1/2}(\log n)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\\
&\lesssim[n/(\log n)^{3}]^{-1/(4d+1)}.\end{split}

The bound on
\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\|F_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}-F_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}
proceeds similarly noting that

\begin{split}F_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}(y)&=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{y}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath tx}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\phi_{n}(t\mathsf{v})\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=F_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}(y)+\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{n}}\int_{-\infty}^{y}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath tx}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\mathbb{G}_{n}(e^{\imath t\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}})\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath ty}\frac{[\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})-1]}{-\imath t}\hat{\mu}_{0Y}(t\mathsf{v})\,\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

so that

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|F_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}(y)-F_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}(y)|\,\mathrm{d}y&\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath ty}\frac{[\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})-1]}{-\imath t}\hat{\mu}_{0Y}(t\mathsf{v})\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg|\,\mathrm{d}y\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{-\infty}^{y}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath tx}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\mathbb{G}_{n}(e^{\imath t\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}})\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg|\,\mathrm{d}y\\
&=:\bar{B}_{1}(\mathsf{v})+\bar{B}_{2}(\mathsf{v}).\end{split}

The first term
\bar{B}_{1}(\mathsf{v})
is similar to
B_{1}(\mathsf{v})
, therefore

\bar{B}_{1}(\mathsf{v})\lesssim b_{n}

uniformly in
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
We now study the term
\bar{B}_{2}(\mathsf{v})
following the control of
B_{2}
in Section
G.2
below. First note that

\bar{B}_{2}(\mathsf{v})=(2\pi\sqrt{n})^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\int_{-\infty}^{y}g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\,\mathrm{d}x)|\,\mathrm{d}y
,
where the function
g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})
is defined in (
G.2
). Set
G_{y,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}):=\int_{-\infty}^{y}g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\,\mathrm{d}x
,
y\in\mathbb{R}
, write

\bar{B}_{2}(\mathsf{v})=\bigg(\int_{-\infty}^{0}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg)\big|\mathbb{G}_{n}\big(G_{y,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\big)\big|\,\mathrm{d}y.

We only study the case where
y\leq 0
because the case
y>0
can be treated similarly. Without loss of generality, we assume that
v_{j}>0
for all
j\in J^{*}_{d}(\mathsf{v})
.
Since

g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})=(2\pi)\mathop{\scalebox{1.1}{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\circledast$}}}_{j\in J^{*}_{d}(\mathsf{v})}K_{b_{n}v_{j}}(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}),\quad x\in\mathbb{R},

\begin{split}|G_{y,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})|\lesssim\int_{-\infty}^{y}\mathop{\scalebox{1.1}{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\circledast$}}}_{j\in J^{*}_{d}(\mathsf{v})}|K_{b_{n}v_{j}}(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y})|\,\mathrm{d}x&=\int_{-\infty}^{y-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}}\mathop{\scalebox{1.1}{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\circledast$}}}_{j\in J^{*}_{d}(\mathsf{v})}|K_{b_{n}v_{j}}(u)|\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&\lesssim\mathsf{P}\left(\sum_{j\in J^{*}_{d}(\mathsf{v})}b_{n}v_{j}Z_{j}\leq y-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{j\in J^{*}_{d}(\mathsf{v})}\mathsf{P}\left(v_{j}Z_{j}\leq(y+\|\mathsf{Y}\|_{1})/(db_{n})\right),\end{split}

where the
Z_{j}
’s are i.i.d. random variables with density
|K|/\|K\|_{1}
.
Hence, for all
k>1
, defined
\mathsf{v}_{\min}:=\min_{j\in[d]}|v_{j}|
, we have

\begin{split}G_{y,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})&\leq\mathbbm{1}_{(y\leq(-2\|\mathsf{Y}\|_{1}\wedge-1))}d\mathsf{P}\left(Z_{1}\leq y/(2db_{n}\mathsf{v}_{\min})\right)+d\mathbbm{1}_{(0\geq y>(-2\|\mathsf{Y}\|_{1}\wedge-1))}\\
&\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{(y\leq-1)}(b_{n}/|y|)^{k-1}+\mathbbm{1}_{(-1<y\leq 0)},\end{split}

which in turns implies that

\mathbb{E}_{0Y}(G_{y,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})^{2})\leq\mathbbm{1}_{(y\leq-1)}(b_{n}/|y|)^{2(k-1)}+\mathbbm{1}_{(-1<y\leq 0)}.

Using Lemma 19.36 of
[
vaart_1998
]
, p. 268, jointly with the computations of the integrated bracketing entropy in Section
G.2
,

\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\left(\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\bar{B}_{2}(\mathsf{v})\right)\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}.

This implies that

\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\|F_{\tilde{\mu}_{Yn,\mathsf{v}}}-F_{\mu_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}}\|_{1}\lesssim b_{n},

which concludes the proof.

G.2
Proof of the bounds in (
G.1
)

We begin to prove the bound on
B_{1}(\mathsf{v})
.

\bullet

Bound on
B_{1}(\mathsf{v})

For
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let

a_{\mathsf{v}}(x):=(2\pi)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath tx}\hat{\mu}_{0Y}(t\mathsf{v})[1-\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})]\,\mathrm{d}t
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Using the inequality

\|a_{\mathsf{v}}\|_{1}^{2}\leq\|\hat{a}_{\mathsf{v}}\|_{2}\times\|\hat{a}_{\mathsf{v}}^{(1)}\|_{2}
, see,
e.g.
, (4.4) in
[
Bobkov:2016
]
, p. 1030, we have

\begin{split}[B_{1}(\mathsf{v})]^{2}&\leq\|\hat{\mu}_{0Y}(\cdot\mathsf{v})[1-\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(\cdot\mathsf{v})]\|_{2}\times\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\hat{\mu}_{0Y}(\cdot\mathsf{v})[1-\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(\cdot\mathsf{v})]\right)\right\|_{2}.\end{split}

Note that

\begin{split}|1-\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})|&\leq\sum_{j\in J_{d}^{*}(\mathsf{v})}|1-\hat{K}(b_{n}v_{j}t)|\leq d\mathbbm{1}_{\left(|t|\geq(b_{n}\|\mathsf{v}\|_{\infty}\right)^{-1})}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\hat{\mu}_{0Y}(t\mathsf{v})[1-\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})]\right)\right|&\lesssim\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{(|t|\geq(b_{n}\|\mathsf{v}\|_{\infty})^{-1})}}{\prod_{j=1}^{d}(1+v_{j}^{2}t^{2})}\\
&\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,\,\times\left[\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\hat{\mu}_{0X}(t\mathsf{v})\right|+2|\hat{\mu}_{0X}(t\mathsf{v})|\sum_{j\in J_{d}^{*}(\mathsf{v})}\frac{v_{j}^{2}|t|}{1+v_{j}^{2}t^{2}}\right]\\
&\lesssim\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{(|t|\geq(b_{n}\|\mathsf{v}\|_{\infty})^{-1})}}{\prod_{j=1}^{d}[1+v_{j}^{2}/(b_{n}\|\mathsf{v}\|_{\infty})^{2}]}\left(\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\hat{\mu}_{0X}(t\mathsf{v})\right|+|\hat{\mu}_{0X}(t\mathsf{v})|\right).\end{split}

By assumption (
5.2
), recalling inequality (
2.3
), we have

\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\hat{\mu}_{0X}(t\mathsf{v})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}|\mu_{0X,\mathsf{v}}(x)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathsf{x}|^{2}f_{0X}(\mathsf{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{x}=M_{2}(\mu_{0X})<\infty.

Combining previous bounds and recalling that
I_{b_{n}}^{\ast}(\mathsf{v})=\{j\in[d]:\,|v_{j}|>b_{n}\}
, we obtain that

\begin{split}B_{1}(\mathsf{v})&\lesssim\frac{[M_{2}(\mu_{0X})]^{1/2}+\|\hat{\mu}_{0X}(\cdot\mathsf{v})\|_{2}}{\prod_{j=1}^{d}[1+v_{j}^{2}/(b_{n}\|\mathsf{v}\|_{\infty})^{2}]}\lesssim b_{n}^{2|I_{b_{n}}^{\ast}(\mathsf{v})|}\prod_{j\in I^{\ast}_{b_{n}}(\mathsf{v})}v_{j}^{-2}.\end{split}

\bullet

Bound on
B_{2}(\mathsf{v})

Set

g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath t(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y})}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\,\mathrm{d}t,\quad x\in\mathbb{R},

(G.2)

we have

B_{2}(\mathsf{v})=\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))|\,\mathrm{d}x.

We now control
\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}[\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))|]
.
Let
\mathcal{G}_{n}(x):=\{g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}):\,\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\}
. We use Lemma 19.36 of
[
vaart_1998
]
, p. 288.
Since
\|\mathsf{v}\|_{\infty}\geq 1/d
for all
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have
|g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})|<2d/b_{n}
. We now bound
\mathbb{E}_{0Y}[g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})^{2}]
. We have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{0Y}[g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})^{2}]&<\frac{2d}{b_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathop{\scalebox{1.1}{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\circledast$}}}_{j\in J_{d}^{*}(\mathsf{v})}|K_{b_{n}v_{j}}(x-y)|f_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\\
&\leq\frac{2d}{b_{n}}\|f_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}\|_{\infty}\|K\|_{1}^{|J_{d}^{*}(\mathsf{v})|}\leq\frac{2d}{b_{n}}\|K\|_{1}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\|f_{0Y,\mathsf{v}}\|_{\infty}=:\delta_{n}^{2}.\end{split}

For
|\mathsf{v}_{1}-\mathsf{v}_{2}|\leq\tau b_{n}^{2}\epsilon
, with
\tau\in(0,\,1)
, we have

\begin{split}|g_{x,\mathsf{v}_{1}}(\mathsf{Y})-g_{x,\mathsf{v}_{2}}(\mathsf{Y})|&\leq\tau b_{n}^{2}\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}|t||\mathsf{Y}|\mathbbm{1}_{|t|\leq d/b_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\tau\epsilon|\mathsf{Y}|d^{2}.\end{split}

Using that
\mathbb{E}_{0Y}[|\mathsf{Y}|^{2}]<\infty
,
a
\delta_{n}
-bracket covering of
\mathcal{G}_{n}
is obtained by an
(\epsilon\tau b_{n}^{2})
-covering of
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
choosing
\tau
accordingly. Hence

J_{[\,]}(\delta_{n},\mathcal{G}_{n}(x),L^{2}(P_{0Y}))\lesssim\int_{0}^{\delta_{n}}\sqrt{\log(1/b_{n})+\log(1/\epsilon)_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon\lesssim\sqrt{\log n}\delta_{n}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{b_{n}}},

which in turns implies that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{|x|\leq R_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\left[\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))|\right]\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\frac{R_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{nb_{n}}}.

We now study
\int_{|x|>R_{n}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))|\,\mathrm{d}x
.
Consider the event
\Omega_{n}=\{|\mathsf{Y}_{i}|\leq R_{n}/2,\,i\in[n]\}
. Then,
|x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}_{i}|\geq|x|/2
when
|x|\geq R_{n}
, and, for all
k\geq 1
,

g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}_{i})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath t(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}_{i})}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\,\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{2\pi[\imath(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}_{i})]^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath t(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y}_{i})}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\,\mathrm{d}t

so that, since
\hat{K}
is
k
-times continuously differentiable and each derivative is equal to 0 on the boundary of its support,

|g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}_{i})|\lesssim\frac{1}{b_{n}|x|^{k}}.

Also on
\Omega_{n}
,

\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))=\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\mathbbm{1}_{(|\mathsf{Y}|\leq|x|/2)})-\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}_{0Y}[g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\mathbbm{1}_{(|\mathsf{Y}|>|x|/2)}]

and, for
|x|>R_{n}
, with the abuse of notation
c_{2}:=(c_{2}\wedge 1)
,

\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}_{0Y}[|g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})|\mathbbm{1}_{(|\mathsf{Y}|>|x|/2)}]\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{n}}{b_{n}}[P_{0X}(|\mathsf{X}|>|x|/4)+e^{-|x|/4}]\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{n}}{b_{n}}e^{-c_{2}|x|/4}.

Therefore on
\Omega_{n}
,

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\int_{|x|>R_{n}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))|\,\mathrm{d}x&\lesssim\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\int_{|x|>R_{n}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\mathbbm{1}_{|\mathsf{Y}|\leq R_{n}/2})|\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{b_{n}}e^{-c_{2}R_{n}/4}.\end{split}

Using the above construction of a covering of
\mathcal{G}_{n}(x)
with the upper bounds, for
|x|>R_{n}
,

\|g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\mathbbm{1}_{|\mathsf{Y}|\leq R_{n}/2}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{1}{b_{n}|x|^{k}}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\|g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\mathbbm{1}_{|\mathsf{Y}|\leq R_{n}/2}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{b_{n}|x|^{k}},

we obtain

J_{[\,]}(2d/b_{n},\mathcal{G}_{n}(x),L^{2}(P_{0Y}))\lesssim\int_{0}^{2/(b_{n}|x|^{k})}\sqrt{\log(1/b_{n})+\log(1/\epsilon)_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{b_{n}|x|^{k}},

so that

\begin{split}\int_{|x|>R_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\left[\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y})\mathbbm{1}_{|\mathsf{Y}|\leq R_{n}/2})|\right]\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}b_{n}R_{n}^{k-1}},\end{split}

which implies that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{|x|>R_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))|\right]\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}b_{n}R_{n}^{k-1}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{b_{n}}e^{-c_{2}R_{n}/4}.

We also bound

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}&\int_{|x|>R_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{c}}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))|\right]\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{c}}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\int_{|x|>R_{n}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath t(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y})}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\,\mathrm{d}t\right|\,\mathrm{d}x\right].\end{split}

By symmetry of
K
, that is,
K(x)=K(-x)
, if
v_{j}\neq 0
, we have

\int e^{-\imath tb_{n}v_{j}x}\hat{K}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{(b_{n}|v_{j}|)}K(x/(b_{n}|v_{j}|))=:K_{b_{n}v_{j}}(x).

Therefore,

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\imath t(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y})}\hat{K}_{b_{n}}^{\otimes d}(t\mathsf{v})\,\mathrm{d}t\right|=\left|\mathop{\scalebox{1.1}{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\circledast$}}}_{j\in J^{*}_{d}(\mathsf{v})}K_{b_{n}v_{j}}(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y})\right|\leq\mathop{\scalebox{1.1}{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\circledast$}}}_{j\in J_{d}^{*}(\mathsf{v})}|K_{b_{n}v_{j}}(x-\mathsf{v}\cdot\mathsf{Y})|.

We thus obtain

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}&\int_{|x|>R_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{c}}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{x,\mathsf{v}}(\mathsf{Y}))|\right]\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{0Y}^{n}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{c}}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\big\|\mathop{\scalebox{1.1}{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\circledast$}}}_{j=1}^{d}K_{b_{n}v_{j}}\big\|_{1}\right]\leq 2P_{0Y}^{n}(\Omega_{n}^{c})\|K\|_{1}^{d}.\end{split}

Since
P_{0Y}^{n}(\Omega_{n}^{c})\leq e^{-R_{n}/4}+P_{0X}(|\mathsf{X}|>|x|/4)\leq e^{-c_{2}R_{n}/4}
(assuming without loss of generality that
c_{2}\leq 1
), by choosing
R_{n}=R_{0}\log n
, with
R_{0}
large enough, we get that

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}B_{2}(\mathsf{v})&\lesssim\frac{R_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{nb_{n}}}+\frac{2\sqrt{n}}{b_{n}}e^{-c_{2}R_{n}/4}\lesssim\frac{(\log n)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{nb_{n}}}.\end{split}

This concludes the proof.
∎
